Augustin (d. 430): De Catechizandis Rudibus.

  1. Bingham: Antiquities, X. 2.

Zezschwitz (Tüb.): System der christl. Kirchl. Katechetik. Leipzig, vol. I. 1863; vol. II. in 2 Parts, 1869 and 1872.

Joh. Mayer (R.C.): Geschichte des Katechumenats, and der Katechese, in den ersten sechs Jahrh. Kempten, 1866.

A. Weiss (R.C.): Die altkirchliche Pädagogik dargestelit in Katecumenat und Katechese der ersten sechs Jahrh. Freiburg, 1869.

Fr. X. Funk (R. C): Die Katechumenats-classen des christl. Alterthums, in the Tübing. "Theol. Quartalschrift," Tüb. 1883, p. 41–77.

  1. The catechumenate or preparation for baptism was a very important institution of the early church. It dates substantially from apostolic times. Theophilus was "instructed" in the main facts of the gospel history; and Apollos was "instructed" in the way of the Lord.(156) As the church was set in the midst of a heathen world, and addressed herself in her missionary preaching in the first instance to the adult generation, she saw the necessity of preparing the susceptible for baptism by special instruction under teachers called "catechists," who were generally presbyters and deacons.(157) The catechumenate preceded baptism (of adults); whereas, at a later period, after the general introduction of infant baptism, it followed. It was, on the one hand, a bulwark of the church against unworthy members; on the other, a bridge from the world to the church, a Christian novitiate, to lead beginners forward to maturity. The catechumens or hearers(158) were regarded not as unbelievers, but as half-Christians, and were accordingly allowed to attend all the exercises of worship, except the celebration of the sacraments. They embraced people of all ranks, ages, and grades of culture, even philosophers, statesmen, and rhetoricians,—Justin, Athenagoras, Clement of Alexandria, Tertullian, Cyprian, Arnobius, Lactantius, who all embraced Christianity in their adult years.

The Didache contains in the first six chapters, a high-toned moral catechism preparatory to baptism, based chiefly on the Sermon on the Mount.

There was but one or at most two classes of Catechumens. The usual division into three (or four) classes rests on confusion with the classes of Penitents.(159)

The catechetical school of Alexandria was particularly renowned for its highly learned character.

The duration of this catechetical instruction was fixed sometimes at two years(160) sometimes at three,(161) but might be shortened according to circumstances. Persons of decent moral character and general intelligence were admitted to baptism without delay. The Councils allow immediate admission in cases of sickness.

  1. Confirmation(162) was originally closely connected with baptism, as its positive complement, and was performed by the imposition of hands, and the anointing of several parts of the body with fragrant balsam-oil, the chrism, as it was called. These acts were the medium of the communication of the Holy Spirit, and of consecration to the spiritual priesthood. Later, however, it came to be separated from baptism, especially in the case of infants, and to be regarded as a sacrament by itself. Cyprian is the first to distinguish the baptism with water and the baptism with the Spirit as two sacraments; yet this term, sacrament, was used as yet very indefinitely, and applied to all sacred doctrines and rites.

The Western church, after the third century, restricted the power of confirmation to bishops, on the authority of Acts 8:17; they alone, as the successors of the apostles, being able to impart the Holy Ghost. The Greek church extended this function to priests and deacons. The Anglican church retains the Latin practice. Confirmation or some form of solemn reception into full communion on personal profession of faith, after proper instruction, was regarded as a necessary supplement to infant baptism, and afterwards as a special sacrament.

§ 73. Infant Baptism.

On Infant Baptism comp. Just. M.: Dial. c. Tryph. Jud. c. 43. IREN.: Adv. Haer. II. 22, § 4, compared with III. 17, § 1, and other passages. Tertul.: De Baptismo, c. 18. Cypr.: Epist. LIX. ad Fidum. Clem. Alex.: Paedag. III. 217. Orig.: Com. in Rom. V. Opp. IV. 565, and Homil. XIV. in Luc.

See Lit. in vol. I. 463sq., especially Wall. Comp. also W. R. Powers: Irenaeus and Infant Baptism, in the "Am. Presb. and Theol. Rev." N. Y. 1867, pp. 239–267.

While the church was still a missionary institution in the midst of a heathen world, infant baptism was overshadowed by the baptism of adult proselytes; as, in the following periods, upon the union of church and state, the order was reversed. At that time, too, there could, of course, be no such thing, even on the part of Christian parents, as a compulsory baptism, which dates from Justinian’s reign, and which inevitably leads to the profanation of the sacrament. Constantine sat among the fathers at the great Council of Nicaea, and gave legal effect to its decrees, and yet put off his baptism to his deathbed. The cases of Gregory of Nazianzum, St. Chrysostom, and St. Augustin, who had mothers of exemplary piety, and yet were not baptized before early manhood, show sufficiently that considerable freedom prevailed in this respect even in the Nicene and post-Nicene ages. Gregory of Nazianzum gives the advice to put off the baptism of children, where there is no danger of death, to their third year.(163)

At the same time it seems an almost certain fact, though by many disputed, that, with the baptism of converts, the optional baptism of the children of Christian parents in established congregations, comes down from the apostolic age.(164) Pious parents would naturally feel a desire to consecrate their offspring from the very beginning to the service of the Redeemer, and find a precedent in the ordinance of circumcision. This desire would be strengthened in cases of sickness by the prevailing notion of the necessity of baptism for salvation. Among the fathers, Tertullian himself not excepted—for he combats only its expediency—there is not a single voice against the lawfulness and the apostolic origin of infant baptism. No time can be fixed at which it was first introduced. Tertullian suggests, that it was usually based on the invitation of Christ: "Suffer the little children to come unto me, and forbid them not." The usage of sponsors, to which Tertullian himself bears witness, although he disapproves of it, and still more, the almost equally ancient abuse of infant communion, imply the existence of infant baptism. Heretics also practised it, and were not censured for it.

The apostolic fathers make, indeed, no mention of it. But their silence proves nothing; for they hardly touch upon baptism at all, except Hermas, and he declares it necessary to salvation, even for the patriarchs in Hades (therefore, as we may well infer, for children also). Justin Martyr expressly teaches the capacity of all men for spiritual circumcision by baptism; and his "all" can with the less propriety be limited, since he is here speaking to a Jew.(165) He also says that many old men and women of sixty and seventy years of age have been from childhood disciples of Christ.(166) Polycarp was eighty-six years a Christian, and must have been baptized in early youth. According to Irenaeus, his pupil and a faithful bearer of Johannean tradition, Christ passed through all the stages of life, to sanctify them all, and came to redeem, through himself, "all who through him are born again unto God, sucklings, children, boys, youths, and adults."(167) This profound view seems to involve an acknowledgment not only of the idea of infant baptism, but also of the practice of it; for in the mind of Irenaeus and the ancient church baptism and regeneration were intimately connected and almost identified.(168) In an infant, in fact, any regeneration but through baptism cannot be easily conceived. A moral and spiritual regeneration, as distinct from sacramental, would imply conversion, and this is a conscious act of the will, an exercise of repentance and faith, of which the infant is not capable.

In the churches of Egypt infant baptism must have been practised from the first. For, aside from some not very clear expressions of Clement of Alexandria, Origen distinctly derives it from the tradition of the apostles; and through his journeys in the East and West he was well acquainted with the practice of the church in his time.(169)

The only opponent of infant baptism among the fathers is the eccentric and schismatic Tertullian, of North Africa. He condemns the hastening of the innocent age to the forgiveness of sins, and intrusting it with divine gifts, while we would not commit to it earthly property.(170) Whoever considers the solemnity of baptism, will shrink more from the receiving, than from the postponement of it. But the very manner of Tertullian’s opposition proves as much in favor of infant baptism as against it. He meets it not as an innovation, but as a prevalent custom; and he meets it not with exegetical nor historical arguments, but only with considerations of religious prudence. His opposition to it is founded on his view of the regenerating effect of baptism, and of the impossibility of having mortal sins forgiven in the church after baptism; this ordinance cannot be repeated, and washes out only the guilt contracted before its reception. On the same ground he advises healthy adults, especially the unmarried, to postpone this sacrament until they shall be no longer in danger of forfeiting forever the grace of baptism by committing adultery, murder, apostasy, or any other of the seven crimes which he calls mortal sins. On the same principle his advice applies only to healthy children, not to sickly ones, if we consider that he held baptism to be the indispensable condition of forgiveness of sins, and taught the doctrine of hereditary sin. With him this position resulted from moral earnestness, and a lively sense of the great solemnity of the baptismal vow. But many put off baptism to their death-bed, in moral levity and presumption, that they might sin as long as they could.

Tertullian’s opposition, moreover, had no influence, at least no theoretical influence, even in North Africa. His disciple Cyprian differed from him wholly. In his day it was no question, whether the children of Christian parents might and should be baptized—on this all were agreed,—but whether they might be baptized so early as the second or third day after birth, or, according to the precedent of the Jewish circumcision, on the eighth day. Cyprian, and a council of sixty-six bishops held at Carthage in 253 under his lead, decided for the earlier time, yet without condemning the delay.(171) It was in a measure the same view of the almost magical effect of the baptismal water, and of its absolute necessity to salvation, which led Cyprian to hasten, and Tertullian to postpone the holy ordinance; one looking more at the beneficent effect of the sacrament in regard to past sins, the other at the danger of sins to come.

§ 74. Heretical Baptism.

On Heretical Baptism comp. Eusebius: H.E. VII. 3–5. Cyprian: Epist. LXX.-LXXVI. The Acts of the Councils of Carthage, a.d. 255 and 256, and the anonymous tract, De Rebaptismate, among Cyprian’s works, and in Routh’s Reliquiae Sacrae, vol. v. 283–328.

Hefele: Conciliengeschichte, I. 117–132 (second ed.).

G. E. Steitz: Ketzertaufe, in Herzog, rev. ed., VII. 652–661.

Heretical baptism was, in the third century, the subject of a violent controversy, important also for its bearing on the question of the authority of the Roman see.

Cyprian, whose Epistles afford the clearest information on this subject, followed Tertullian(172) in rejecting baptism by heretics as an inoperative mock-baptism, and demanded that all heretics coming over to the Catholic church be baptized (he would not say re-baptized). His position here was due to his high-church exclusiveness and his horror of schism. As the one Catholic church is the sole repository of all grace, there can be no forgiveness of sins, no regeneration or communication of the Spirit, no salvation, and therefore no valid sacraments, out of her bosom. So far he had logical consistency on his side. But, on the other hand, he departed from the objective view of the church, as the Donatists afterwards did, in making the efficacy of the sacrament depend on the subjective holiness of the priest. "How can one consecrate water," he asks, "who is himself unholy, and has not the Holy Spirit?" He was followed by the North African church, which, in several councils at Carthage in the years 255–6, rejected heretical baptism; and by the church of Asia Minor, which had already acted on this view, and now, in the person of the Cappadocian bishop Firmilian, a disciple and admirer of the great Origen, vigorously defended it against Rome, using language which is entirely inconsistent with the claims of the papacy.(173)

The Roman bishop Stephen (253–257) appeared for the opposite doctrine, on the ground of the ancient practice of his church.(174) He offered no argument, but spoke with the consciousness of authority, and followed a catholic instinct. He laid chief stress on the objective nature of the sacrament, the virtue of which depended neither on the officiating priest, nor on the receiver, but solely on the institution of Christ. Hence he considered heretical baptism valid, provided only it was administered with intention to baptize and in the right form, to wit, in the name of the Trinity, or even of Christ alone; so that heretics coming into the church needed only confirmation or the ratification of baptism by the Holy Ghost. "Heresy," says he, "produces children and exposes them; and the church takes up the exposed children, and nourishes them as her own, though she herself has not brought them forth."

The doctrine of Cyprian was the more consistent from the hierarchical point of view; that of Stephen, from the sacramental. The former was more logical, the latter more practical and charitable. The one preserved the principle of the exclusiveness of the church; the other, that of the objective force of the sacrament, even to the borders of the opus operatum theory. Both were under the direction of the same churchly spirit, and the same hatred of heretics; but the Roman doctrine is after all a happy inconsistency of liberality, an inroad upon the principle of absolute exclusiveness, an involuntary concession, that baptism, and with it the remission of sin and regeneration, therefore salvation, are possible outside of Roman Catholicism.(175)

The controversy itself was conducted with great warmth. Stephen, though advocating the liberal view, showed the genuine papal arrogance and intolerance. He would not even admit to his presence the deputies of Cyprian, who brought him the decree of the African synod, and he called this bishop, who in every respect excelled Stephen, and whom the Roman church now venerates as one of her greatest saints, a false Christ and false apostle.(176) He broke off all intercourse with the African church, as he had already with the Asiatic. But Cyprian and Firmilian, nothing daunted, vindicated with great boldness, the latter also with bitter vehemence, their different view, and continued in it to their death. The Alexandrian bishop Dionysius endeavored to reconcile the two parties, but with little success. The Valerian persecution, which soon ensued, and the martyrdom of Stephen (257) and of Cyprian (258), suppressed this internal discord.

In the course of the fourth century, however, the Roman theory gradually gained on the other, received the sanction of the oecumenical Council of Nicaea in 325, was adopted in North Africa during the Donatistic controversies, by a Synod of Carthage, 348, defended by the powerful dialectics of St. Augustin against the Donatists, and was afterwards confirmed by the Council of Trent with an anathema on the opposite view.

Note.

The Council of Trent declares (Sessio Sept., March 3, 1547, canon 4): "If any one says that the baptism, which is even given by heretics in the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Ghost, with the intention of doing what the church doth, is not true baptism: let him be anathema." The Greek church likewise forbids the repetition of baptism which has been performed in the name of the Holy Trinity, but requires trine immersion. See the Orthodox Conf. Quaest. CII. (in Schaff’s Creeds II. 376), and the Russian Catch. (II. 493), which says: "Baptism, is spiritual birth: a man is born but once, therefore he is also baptized but once." But the same Catechism declares "trine immersion" to be "most essential in the administration of baptism"(II. 491).

The Roman church, following the teaching of St. Augustin, bases upon the validity of heretical and schismatical baptism even a certain legal claim on all baptized persons, as virtually belonging to her communion, and a right to the forcible conversion of heretics under favorable circumstances.(177) But as there may be some doubt about the orthodox form and intention of heretical baptism in the mind of the convert (e.g. if he be a Unitarian), the same church allows a conditional rebaptism with the formula: "If thou art not yet baptized, I baptize thee," etc.

Evangelical creeds put their recognition of Roman Catholic or any other Christian baptism not so much on the theory of the objective virtue of the sacrament, as on a more comprehensive and liberal conception of the church. Where Christ is, there is the church, and there are true ordinances. The Baptists alone, among Protestants, deny the validity of any other baptism but by immersion (in this respect resembling the Greek church), but are very far on that account from denying the Christian status of other denominations, since baptism with them is only a sign (not a means) of regeneration or conversion, which precedes the rite and is independent of it.

Eusebius; reports (Vita Const. III. 19) that especially the province of Asia acknowledged the decree. (102; original 391): Leitourgiva tw'n kathcoumevnwn, Missa Catechumenorum. The name missa (from which our mass is derived) occurs first in Augustin and in the acts of the council of Carthage, a.d. 398. it arose from the formula of dismission at the close of each part of the service, and is equivalent to missio, dismissio. Augustin (Serm. 49, c. 8): "Take notice, after the sermon the dismissal (missa) of the catechumens takes place; the faithful will remain." Afterwards missa came to designate exclusively the communion service. In the Greek church leitourgiva or litourgiva, service, is the precise equivalent for missa.

(114; original 403): Cyprian speaks of daily sacrifice, ;. Ep. 54: "Sacerdotes qui Sacrificia Dei quotidie celebramus." So Ambrose, Ep. 14 ad Marcell., and the oldest liturgical works. (115; original 404): Rom. 16:16; 1 Cor. 16:20; 2 Cor. 13:12; 1 Thess. 5:26; 1 Pet. 5:14. The Kiss of Peace continued in the Latin church till the end of the thirteenth century, and was then transferred to the close of the service or exchanged for a mere form of words: Pax tibi et ecclesiae. In the Russian church the clergy kiss each other during the recital of the Nicene Creed to show the nominal union of orthodoxy and charity (so often divided). In the Coptic church the primitive custom is still in force, and in some small Protestant sects it has been revived.

(118; original 407): Koino;" a[rto", says; Justin, while in view of its sacred import be calls it also uncommon bread and drink. The use of leavened or unleavened bread became afterwards, as is well known, a point of controversy between the Roman and Greek churches.

(119; original 408): This simplest form of distribution, "Sw'ma Cristou'," and "Ai[ma Cr., pothvrion zwh'"" occurs in the Clementine liturgy of the Apostolic Constitutions, VIII. 13, and seems to be the oldest. The Didache gives no form of distribution.

(120; original 409): The standing posture of the congregation during the principal prayers, and in the communion itself, seems to have been at first universal. For this was, indeed, the custom always on the day of the resurrection in distinction from Friday ("stantes oramus, quod est signunt resurrectionis," says Augustin) besides, the communion was, in the highest sense, a ceremony of festivity and joy; and finally, Justin expressly observes: "Then we all stand up to prayer." After the twelfth century, kneeling in receiving the elements became general, and passed from the Catholic church into the Lutheran and Anglican, while most of the Reformed churches; returned to the original custom of standing. Sitting in the communion was first introduced after the Reformation by the Presbyterian church of Scotland, and is very common in the United States the deacons or elders banding the bread and cup to the communicants in their pews. A curious circumstance is the sitting posture of the Pope in the communion, which Dean Stanley regards as a relic of the reclining or recumbent posture of the primitive disciples. See his Christ. Instit. p. 250 sqq.

(121; original 410): On Maundy-Thursday, according, to Augustin’s testimony, the communion continued to be celebrated in the evening, "tanquam ad insigniorem commemorationem." So on high feasts, as Christmas night, Epiphany, and Easter Eve, and in fasting seasons. See Ambrose, Serm. viii. in Ps. 118.

(122; original 411): Apol. c.39: "About the modest supper-room of the Christians alone a great ado is made. Our feast explains itself by its name. The Greeks call it love. Whatever it costs, our outlay in the name of piety is gain, since with the good things of the feast we benefit the needy, not as it is with you, do parasites aspire to the glory of satisfying their licentious propensities, selling themselves for a belly-feast to all disgraceful treatment-but as it is with God himself, a peculiar respect is shown to the lowly. If the object of our feast be good, in the light of that consider its further regulations. As it is an act of religious service, it permits no vileness or immodesty. The participants, before reclining, taste first of prayer to God. As much is eaten as satisfies the cravings of hunger; as much is drunk as befits the chaste. They say it is enough, as those who remember that even during the night they have to worship God; they talk as those who know that the Lord is one of their auditors. After the washing of hands and the bringing in of lights, each is asked to stand forth and sing, as he can, a hymn to God, either one from the holy Scriptures or one of his own composing-a proof of the measure of our drinking. As the feast commenced with prayer, so with prayer it closed. We go from it, not like troops of mischief-doers, nor bands of roamers, nor to break out into licentious acts, but to have aq ruucli care of our modesty and chastity as if we had been at a school of virtue rather than a banquet." (Translation from the "Ante-Nicene Library ").

(129; original 418): Adv. Marc. IV. 40; and likewise III. 19. Quando autem utrumque miscetur et adunatione confusa sibi invicem copitlatur, tunc sacramentum spirituale et cœleste perficitur."

(132; original 421): Comment. ser. in Matt. c. 85 (III. 898): "Panis iste, quem Dem Verbum [Logos] corpus suum esse fatetur, verbum est nutritorium animarum, verbum de Deo Verbo procedens, et panis de pani cœlesti ... Non enim panem illum visibilem, quem tenebat in manibus, corpus situm dicebat Deus Verbum, sed verbum, in cuius mysterio est panis ille frangendus." Then the same of the wine. (138; original 427): Abrenunciatio diaboti. Tertullian: "Renunciare diabolo et pompae et angelis ejus." Const. Apost.: jApotavssomai tw/' Satana/' kai; toi'" e[rgoi" aujtou' kai; tai;" pompai'" aujtou', kai; tai'" latreivai" aujtou', kai; pa'si toi'" uJp j aujtovn This renunciation of the devil was made, at least in the fourth century, as we learn from Cyril of Jerusalem, in the vestibule of the baptistery, with the face towards the west, and the hand raised in the repelling posture, as if Satan were present (wJ" parovnti ajpotavssesqe Satana/'), and was sometimes accompanied with exsufflations, or other signs of expulsion of the evil spirit.

(139; original 428): JOmolovghsi", professio. The creed was either said by the catechumen after The priest, or confessed in answer to questions, and with the face turned eastwards towards the light.

(140; original 429): See the authorities (Quoted in Smith and Cheetham, I. 161, and more fully in Augusti.. l.c."Ter mergitamur, " says Tertullian. Immersion was very natural in Southern climates. The baptisteries of the Nicene age, of which many remain in Asia, Africa, and Southern Europe, were built for immersion, and all Oriental churches still adhere to this mode. Garrucci (Storia della Arte Cristiana, I. 27) says: "Antichissimo e solenne fu il rito d’ immergere la persona nell’ acqua, e tre volte anche it capo, al pronunziare del ministro i tre nomi." Schultze (Die Katacomben, p. 136): "Die Taufdarstellungen vorkonstantinischer Zeit, deren Zahl sich auf drei beläuft, zeigen sämmtlich erwachsene Täuflinge, in zvei FälIen Knabent von etwa zwölf Jahren, im dritten Falle einen Jüngling. Der Act wird durch Untertauchen vollzogen." Dean Stanley delights in pictorial exaggeration of the baptismal immersion in patristic times as contrasted with modern sprinkling. "Baptism," he says, "was not only a bath, but a plunge—an entire submersion in the deep water, a leap as into the rolling sea or the rushing river, where for the moment the waves close over the bather’s head, and he emerges again as from a momentary grave; or it was a shock of a shower-bath—the rush of water passed over the whole person from capacious vessels, so as to wrap the recipient as within the veil of a splashing cataract. This was the part of the ceremony on which the Apostles laid so much stress. It was to them like a burial of the old former self and the rising up again of the new self."Christian Institutions, (1881), p. 9. See Schaff, l.c. p. 41 sqq.

(141; original 430): Ep. I. 41 in reply to Leander, bishop of Hispala. Thomas Aquinas (Summa Theol., Tom. IV., f. 615, ed. Migne) quotes this letter with approval, but gives the preference to trina immersio, as expressing "triduum sepulturus Christi et etiam Trinitas personarum."

(142; original 431): The Russian Orthodox Catechism defines baptism as "a sacrament, in which a man who believes, having his body thrice plunged in water in the name of God the Father, the Son, and the Holy Ghost, dies to the carnal life of sin, and is born again of the Holy Ghost to a life spiritual and holy." In the case of infants the act is usually completed by pouring water over the head, the rest of the body being, immersed. So I was informed by a Greek priest.

(143; original 432): Pouring or affusion is the present practice of the Roman Catholic church. It is first found on pictures in the Roman catacombs, one of which De Rossi assigns to the second century (in the cemetry of Calixtus). "It is remarkable that in almost all the earliest representations of baptism that have been preserved to us, this [the pouring of water from vessels over the body] is the special act represented." Marriott in Smith and Cheetham, I. 168. But the art of painting can only represent a part of the act, not the whole process; in all the Catacomb pictures the candidate stands with the feet in water, and is undressed as for immersion, total or partial.

(144; original 433): "Baptismus clinicorum" (klinikoiv, from klvivnhbed) Clinicus or grabbatarius designated one who was baptized on the sick bed.

(145; original 434): Ep. 69 (al. 75), ad Magnum. He answered the question as best be could in the absence of any ecclesiastical decision at that time. This Epistle, next to Tertullian’s opposition to infant baptism, is the oldest document in the controversial baptismal literature. Cyprian quotes (ch. 12) several passages from the O.T. where "sprinkling" is spoken of as an act of cleansing (Ez. 36:25, 26; Num. 8:5–7; 19:8–13), and then concludes: "Whence it appears that sprinkling also of water prevails equally with the salutary washing (adspersionem quoque aquae instar salutaris lavacri obtinere); and that when this is done in the church where the faith both of the receiver and the giver is sound (ubi sit et accipieatis et dantis fides integra), all things hold and may be consummated and perfected by the majesty of the Lord and by the truth of faith." But in the same Ep., Cyprian denies the validity of heretical and schismatic baptism in any form. See below, §74.

(146; original 435): The twelfth canon of the Council of Neo-Caesarea (after 314) ordains: "Whosoever has received clinical baptism cannot be promoted to the priesthood, because his [profession of] faith was not from free choice, but from necessity (ejx ajnavgkh" ,fear of death), unless he, excel afterwards in zeal and faith, or there is a deficiency of [able] men." This canon passed into the Corpus jur. can. c. 1 Dist. 57. See Hefele, Conciliengesch, I. 249 (2nd ed.).

(147; original 436): Pouring and sprinkling were still exceptional in the ninth century according to Walafrid Strabo (De Rel. Eccl., c. 26), but they made gradual progress with the spread of infant baptism, as the most convenient mode, especially in Northern climates, and came into common use in the West at the end of the thirteenth century, Thomas Aquinas (d. 1274) says, that although it may be safer to baptize by immersion, yet pouring and sprinkling are also allowable (Summa Theol. P. III. Qu. LXVI. De Rapt. art. 7: in Migne’s ed. Tom. IV. fol. 614): "Si totum corpus aquâ non possit perfundi propter aquae paucitatem, vel propter aliquam aliam causam, opportet caput perfundere, in quo manifestatur principium animalis vitae. In Ireland aspersion seems to have been practiced very early along with immersion." Trine immersion, with the alternative of aspersion, is ordered in the earliest extant Irish Baptismal Office, in the composition of which, however, Roman influence is strongly marked." F. E. Warren, The Liturgy and Ritual of the CeItic Church, Oxford (Clarendon Press), 1881, p. 65. Prof. Norman Fox and other Baptist writer., ;, think that " neither infant baptism nor the use of pouring and sprinkling for baptism would ever have been thought of but for the superstitious idea that baptism was necessary to salvation."But this idea prevailed among the fathers and in the Greek church fully as much as in the Roman, while it is rejected in most Protestant churches where sprinkling is practiced.

Luther sought to restore immersion, but without effect. Calvin took a similar view of the subject as Thomas Aquinas, but he went farther and declared the mode of application to be a matter of indifference, Inst. IV. ch. 15, §19: " Whether the person who is baptized be wholly immersed (mergatur totus)and whether thrice or once, or whether water be only poured (infusa)or sprinkled upon him (aspergatur), is of no importance (minimum refert): but this should be left free to the churches according to the difference of countries. Yet the very word baptize signifies to immerse (mergere); and it is certain that immersion was the practice of the ancient church." Most Protestants agree with Calvin, except the Baptists, who revived the ancient practice, but only in part (single instead of trine immersion), and without the patristic ideas of baptismal regeneration, infant baptism, and the necessity of baptism for salvation. They regard baptism as a mere symbol which exhibits the fact that regeneration and conversion have already taken place.

(148; original 437): Tertullian calls it "principals crimen generis humani" (De idol. c. 1), and Cyprian, "summum delictum" (Ep. x.).

(149; original 438): jAnavdocoi, sponsores, fideijussores.

(150; original 439): The patristic terms for baptism expressive of doctrine are ajnagevnnhsi", paliggenesiva(and loutro;n paliggenesiva" ,Tit. 3:5), qeogevnesi"regeneratio, secunda or spiritualis nativitas, renascentia; also fwtismov" , fwvtisma, illuminatio, sfragiv",signaculum, seal, muvhsi", mustagwgiva, initiation into the mysteries (the sacraments). The sign was almost identified with the thing itself.

(151; original 440): "Non defectus (or privatio), sed contemtus sacramenti damnat." This leaves the door open for the salvation of Quakers, unbaptized children, and elect heathen who die with a desire for salvation.

(152; original 441): Procrastinatio baptismi.

(153; original 442): So the author of the Apost. Constit., VI. 15, disapproves those who say: o{tio{tan teleutw', baptivzomai, i{na mh; aJmarthvsw kai; rJupanw' to; bavptisma.

(154; original 443): De Paenitientia.

(155; original 444): De Opere et Eleemosynis.

(156; original 445): Luke 1:4 (kathchvqh") Acts 18:25 (kathchmevno"); Comp. Rom. 2:18; 1 Cor.14:19; Gal. 6:6; Heb. 5:12. The verb kathcevw means 1) to resound; 2) to teach by word of mouth; 3) in Christian writers, to instruct in the elements of religion.

(157; original 446): Kathchtaiv, doctores audientium. The term designates a function, not a special office or class.

(158; original 447): Kathcouvmenoi, jakroataiv, auditores, audientes.

(159; original 448): jAkrowvmenoi, or audientes; gonuklivnonte", or genuflectentes; and fwtizovmenoi, or competentes. So Ducange, Augusti, Neander, Höfling, Hefele (in the first ed. of his Conciliengesch., but modified in the second, vol. I. 246, 249), Zezschwitz, Herzog, and many others. Bona and Bingham add even a fourth class (ejxwqouvmenoi). But this artificial classification (as Dr. Funk has shown, l.c.) arose from a misunderstanding of the fifth canon of Neocaesarea (between 314 and 325), which mentions one govnu klivnwn, but as representing a class of penitents, not of catechumens. Suicer, Mayer, and Weiss assume but two classes, audientes and competentes. Funk maintains that the candidates for baptism (fwtizovmenoi, companies or electi baptizandi) were already numbered among the faithful (fideles), and that there was only one class of catechumens.

(160; original 449): Conc. of Elvira, can. 42

(161; original 450): Const. Apost. VIII. 32.

(162; original 451): Sfragiv", crivsma, confirmatio obsignatio, signaculum.

(163; original 452): Orat. XL.

(164; original 453): Comp. I. 469 sq. The fact is not capable of positive proof, but rests on strong probabilities. The Baptists deny it. So does Neander, but lie approves the practice of infant baptism as springing from the spirit of Christianity.

(165; original 454): Dial. c. Tr. c. 43.

(166; original 455): Apol. l.c. 15 (Otto 1. 48): oiJ ejk paivdwn ejmaqhteuvqhsan tw'/ Cristw'/

(167; original 456): Adv. Haer. II. 22, § 4: "Omnes venit per semetipsum salvare; omnes, inquam qui per cum renascuntur in Deum, infantes et parvulos et pueros et juvenes et seniores. Ideo per omnem venit aetatem, et infantibus infans factus, sanctificans infantes; in parvulis parvulus, sanctificans hanc ipsam habentes aetatem; simul et exemplunt illis pietatis effectus et justitae et subjectionis, in juvenibus juvenis," etc. Neander, in discussing this passage remarks, that" from this idea, founded on what is inmost in Christianity, becoming prominent in the feeling of Christians, resulted the practice of infant baptism" (I. 312, Boston ed.)

(168; original 457): Irenaeus speaks of "the washing of regeneration, " and of the "baptism of regeneration unto God,"to; bavptisma th'" eij" qeo;n ajnagennhvsew" (Adv. Haer. l.c. 21, § 1); he identifies the apostolic commission to baptize with the potestas regenerationis in Deum (III. 17, § 1); he says that Christ descending into Hades, regenerated the ancient patriarchs (III. c. 22, § 4; "in sinum suum recipiens pristinos patres regeneravit eos in vitam Dei"), by which he probably meant baptism (according to the fancy of Hermas, Clement of Alex., and others). Compare an examination of the various passages of Irenaeus in the article by Powers, who comes to the conclusion (l.c. p. 267) that " Irenaeus everywhere implies baptism in the regeneration he so often names."

(169; original 458): In Ep. ad Rom. (Opera, vol. IV. col. 1047 ed. Migne; or IV. 565 ed. Delarue): "Pro hoc et Ecclesia ab apostolis traditionem suscepit, etiam parvulis baptismum dare." In Levit. Hom. VIII. (II. 496 in Migne), he says that "secundum Ecclesiae observantiam" baptism was given also to children (etiam parvulis). Comp. his Com. in Matt. XV. (III. 1268 sqq.) where he seems to infer this custom from the example of Christ blessing little children. That Origen himself was baptized in childhood (185 or soon after), is nowhere expressly stated in his works (as far as I know), but may be inferred as probable from his descent of, and early religious instruction, by Christian parents (reported by Euseb H. E. VI. 19: tw'/jOrigevnei ta; th'" kata; Cristo;n didaskaliva" ejk progovnwn ejswvzeto), in connection with the Egyptian custom. Comp. Redepenning, Origenes, I. 49. It would certainly be more difficult to prove that be was not baptized in infancy. He could easily make room for infant baptism in his theological system, which involved the Platonic idea of a prehistoric fall of the individual soul. But the Cyprianic and Augustinian theology connected it with the historic fall of Adam, and the consequent hereditary depravity and guilt.

(170; original 459): ’Quid festinat innocens aetas ad remissionem peccatorum?" The" innocens" here is to be taken only in a relative sense; for Tertullian in other plain teaches a vitium originis, or hereditary sin and guilt, although not as distinctly and clearly as Augustin

(171; original 460): A later council of Carthage of the year 418 went further and decreed: "item placuit, ut quicunque parvulos recentes ab uteris matrum baptizandos negat ... anathema sit."

(172; original 461): De Bapt. c. 15. Comp. also Clement of Alex., Strom. I. 375.

(173; original 462): See p. 162. Some Roman divines (Molkenkuhr and Tizzani, as quoted by Hefele, p. 121) thought that such an irreverent Epistle as that of Firmilian (the 75th among Cyprian’s Epp.) cannot be historical, and that the whole story of the controversy between Pope Stephen and St. Cyprian must be a fabrication! Dogma versus facts

(174; original 463): According to Hippolytus (Philosoph.), the rebaptism of heretics was unknown before Callistus, a.d. 218-223. Cyprian does not deny the antiquity of the Roman customs but pleads that truth is better than custom ("quasi consuetudo major sit veritate"). Hefele, 1. p. 121. The Epistles of Stephen are lost, and we must learn his position from his opponents.

(175; original 464): Unless it be maintained that the baptismal grace, if received outside of the Catholic communion, is of no use, but rather increases the guilt (like the knowledge of the heathen), and become, ; available only by the subjective conversion and regular confirmation of the heretic. This was the view of Augustin; see Steitz, l. c., p. 655 sq.

(176; original 465): "Pseudochristum, pseudoapostolum, et dolosum operarium." Firmil. Ad Cyp. toward, ; the end (Ep. 75). Hefele (I. 120) calls this unchristian intolerance of Stephen very mildly "eine grosse Unfreundlichkeit."

(177; original 466): Augustin thus misinterpreted the "Coge intrare,"Luke 14:22, 23, as justifying persecution (Ep. ad Bonifac., c. 6). If the holy bishop of Hippo had foreseen the fearful consequences of his exegesis, be would have shrunk from it in horror.

  1. Chorus（唱诗班席），βῆμα*。两者有时被视为同一部分，有时则加以区别：bema 是举行神圣奥秘礼仪的圣所本部，唱诗班席则是圣坛区内供教牧人员使用的其余部分；中殿供平信徒使用。↩︎

  2. Ἄμβων*, suggestus, pulpitum（讲坛）。↩︎

  3. Τράπεζα*，mensa sacra（圣桌）；也称 ara, altare（祭坛）。↩︎

  4. Ἐκκλησία, ἐκκλησιαστήριον, κυριακά, οἶκος θεοῦ,*, ecclesia, dominica, domus Dei, templum（教会、属主之所、上帝之家、圣殿）。条顿语族和斯拉夫语族中表示教会建筑的名称（Kirche, Church, Kerk, Kyrka, Tserkoff, 等），经由哥特语，源出希腊文 κυριακή, κυριακόν*（属主的、主的家）；罗曼语族中的名称（Chiesa, Igreja, Eglise, 等）则源出拉丁文 ecclesia，不过这个词也来自希腊文，原意是集会（或指地方会众，或指全体基督徒）。专为纪念殉道者而建的教堂称为 martyria、memoriae、tropaea, tituli。↩︎

  5. In ecclcsima, in domum Dei venire（到教会、到上帝的家中来）。↩︎

  6. Τόπος*, 以及 ἄθροισμα τῶν ἐκλεκτῶν*。↩︎

  7. 《逼迫者之死》(De Mort. Persec.)，第12章。《埃德萨编年史》(Chronicle of Edessa，载 Assem. Bibl Orient. XI. 397) 提到公元292年基督教圣殿遭毁之事。↩︎

  8. 《教会史》(Hist. Ecel.) X. 4。优西比乌 (Eusebius) 也以夸张而不严谨的修辞，描写了君士坦丁 (Constantine) 在耶路撒冷、安提阿和君士坦丁堡所建的教堂（《君士坦丁传》[Vita Const.]，第III卷50；IV. 58、59）。参见德·沃盖 (De Vogüe)，《圣地教堂》(Eglises de la terre-sainte)；许布施 (Hübsch)，前引书；以及史密斯与奇瑟姆 (Smith & Cheetliam)，I. 368及下页。↩︎

    1. 57，乌尔岑 (Ueltzen) 编，第66页及下页。
    ↩︎

  10. 基督教安息日或每周休息日最初的名称是：ἡ μία or μία σαββάτων*，即一周的第一日（太28:1；可16:2；路24:1；约21:1；徒20:7；林前16:2）；以及 ἡ ἡμέρα κυριακή*，即主日，最早见于启1:10，后来见于伊格那丢 (Ignatius) 和教父们的著作。拉丁作者将其译为 Dominicus 或 Dominica dies。巴拿巴 (Barnabas) 称之为第八日，与犹太人的安息日相对。君士坦丁以后，犹太人的名称安息日和异教徒的名称太阳日（ἡμέρα τοῦ ἡλίου*, dies Solis）也都得到使用。在格拉提安 (Gratian) 公元386年的敕令中，两者合用：“Solis die, quem Dominicum rite` dixere majores.”（在太阳日，即先人恰当地称为主日的那一天。）在欧洲大陆，Sunday 已完全排除了 Sabbath；而在英格兰、苏格兰和美国的宗教文献中，Sabbath 的使用与另一名称同样频繁，甚至更为频繁。这一差异，正体现了欧洲大陆与英美在守主日方式上的区别。↩︎

  11. 《书信》(Ep.)，第15章：“我们欢喜庆祝第八日，耶稣在这一天从死里复活，并且在显现［给他的门徒］之后，升到天上。”不能由此推断，巴拿巴也把基督升天的日子定在星期日。↩︎

  12. 《致马格尼西亚人书》(Ep. ad Magnes.)，第8、9章。↩︎

  13. 《第一护教辞》(Apol. I.)，67。↩︎

  14. “Stato die”（在指定的日子），见他致图拉真 (Trajan) 的信，Ep. X. 97。比提尼亚 (Bithynia) 的基督徒在这个“指定的日子”天亮以前聚集，向基督唱诗，奉他为神，并以 sacramentum（誓约）约束自己；这日必定就是主日。↩︎

  15. 第14章：Κυριακὴ κυρίου*，属赘语。这个形容词见于启1:10。↩︎

  19. 《对话》(Dial.) 12（II，第46页）：σαββατίζειν ὑμᾶς*（奥托如此读，但作 ἡμᾶς* 更好）ὁ καινὸς νόμος διὰ παντὸς*（修饰 σαββατίζειν*）ἐθέλει*。比较德尔图良 (Tertullian)《驳犹太人》(Contra Jud.)，第4章：“Unde nos intelligimis magis, sabbatizare nos ab omni opere servili semper debere, et non tantum septimo quoque die, sed per omne tempus.”（由此我们更明白，我们应当始终停止一切奴役性的工作而守安息，不仅每逢第七日如此，而且在一切时候都当如此。）↩︎

  20. 《第一护教辞》(Apol. I.)，67（I，第161页）：Τὴν δὲ τοῦ ἡλίου ἡμέραν κοινῇ πάντες τὴν συνέλευσιν ποιούμεθα, ἐπειδὴ πρώτη ἐστὶν ἡμέρα, ἐν ᾗ ὁ θεὸς τὸ σκότος καὶ τὴν ὕλην τρέψας, κόσμον ἐποίησε, καὶ Ἰησοῦς Χριστὸς ὁ ἡμέτερος σωτὴρ τῇ αὐτῇ ἡμέρᾳ ἐκ νεκρῶν ἀνέστη. κ.τ.λ.*↩︎

  23. 见其一篇 περὶ τοῦ πάσχα* 残篇，以及他参与十四日派争论的事迹；这场争论所涉及的是基督教逾越节的年度庆祝，却隐含着众人对于每周庆祝复活一事的普遍共识。比较赫西 (Hessey)，《论星期日的班普顿讲座》(Bampton Lectures on Sunday)，伦敦，1860年，第373页。↩︎

  24. 《驳异端》(Adv. Haer.) IV. 16。↩︎

  25. 《论祷告》(De Orat.)，第23章：“Nos vero sicut accepimus, solo die Dominicae Resurrectionis non ab isto tantum［屈膝］，sed omni anxietatis habitu et officio cavere debemus, differentes etiam negotia, ne quem diabolo locum demus.”（但照我们所领受的，唯在主复活之日，我们不仅应当避免屈膝，也当避免一切忧虑的姿态和活动，甚至把事务延后，免得给魔鬼留地步。）德尔图良、居普良 (Cyprian)、亚历山大的革利免 (Clement of Alex.) 和奥利金 (Origen) 的其他有关段落，见赫西，前引书，第375页及下页。↩︎

  27. Feria sexta（第六日，即星期五），ἡ παρασκευή*。↩︎

  28. milites Christi（基督的精兵）的 Dies stationum（站岗之日）。↩︎

  29. Semijejunia（半日禁食）。↩︎

  30. Pascha，πάσχα*，并非源于动词 πάσχειν*（受苦；不过，不懂希伯来文的教父们常把它与这个词及拉丁文 passio 混淆），而是源于希伯来文 פֶּסַח*、迦勒底文 פַּסְחָא*（比较动词 פָּסַח*，意为越过、饶恕）。参见《出埃及记》第12、13章；利23:4–9；《民数记》第9章。在《七十士译本》和《新约》中，这个词有三种含义：1）逾越节，又称“除酵节”，从尼散月十四日至二十日，纪念以色列人的长子蒙保全，以及以色列脱离埃及；2）逾越节的羊羔，于尼散月十四日两晚之间（下午3至5时）宰杀；3）同日晚上的逾越节晚餐，这标志着尼散月十五日，即节期第一日的开始。就第一种意义而言，它与基督教的复活节相对应，如同预表与实体相对应。然而，英文译本在徒12:4中把 Passover（逾越节）译为 Easter（复活节），是一个奇怪的时代错置（修订本已予改正）。↩︎

  31. 复活节是纪念复活的节日，在严格意义的逾越节之后，但仍在同一节期的一周之内。英文 Easter（盎格鲁-撒克逊文 easter, eastran，德文 Ostern）与 East（东方）和日出有关，也与 ἠώς*, oriens, aurora 同源（比较雅各布·格林 [Jac. Grimm] 的《德意志神话》[Deutsche Mythol.]，1835年，第181、349页，以及斯基特 [Skeat] 的《英语词源词典》[Etym. Dict. E. Lang.]，Easter 条）。将日出和自然界的春天，比作基督复活中道德上的新创造，并将古代日耳曼人对带来健康的初升光明之神 Ostara 的庆祝，转移到基督教复活节上，原是容易而自然的，因为整个自然界都是属灵事物的象征，而异教神话则是对基督教真理朦胧的预感和属肉体的预期。↩︎

  32. Τὸ μέγα σάββατον, τὸ ἅγιον σάββατον*，Sabbatum magnum（大安息日）。↩︎

  33. Παννυχίδες*, vigiae paschae, Easter Eve（复活节前夕的守夜）。受难日与复活节前夕实行连续禁食，直至午夜或鸡鸣。参见德尔图良，《致妻子》(Ad uxoR.) II. 4；优西比乌，《教会史》(H. E.) VI. 34；《使徒宪章》(Apost. ConSt.) V. 18；VII. 23。↩︎

  34. 名称有多种：πάσχα σταυρώσιμου*（有别于 π. ἀναστάσιμου*）；ἡμέρα σταυροῦ, παρασκευὴ μεγάλη* 或 ἀγία*, parasceue, feria sexta major, Good Friday, Charfreitag（源于 χάρις*，或源于 carus，意为亲爱的）。但这一庆祝似乎并非普遍实行；因为奥古斯丁 (Augustin) 在《致雅努阿里乌斯书》(Ad Januar.) 中说，他不认为这一天是圣日。参见西格尔 (Siegel)，《基督教会古制手册》(Handbuch der christl. Kirchl. Alterthümer)，I. 374及下页。↩︎

  35. 从棕枝主日至复活节前夕。Ἑβδομὰς μεγάλη*，或 τοῦ πάσχα*, hebdomas magna, hebdomas nigra（与 dominica in albis 相对），hebdomas crux, Chaiwoche，即大周、黑周、十字架周等名称。↩︎

  36. 爱任纽 (Irenaeus) 在致罗马的维克托 (Victor) 的信中说（优西比乌，V. 24）：“争议不仅涉及日期，也涉及禁食的方式。有人认为只应禁食一天，有人认为两天，有人认为更多天；有人则把自己的那一天算为昼夜共四十小时。守这节期的人之间所存在的这种差异，并非在我们这个时代才刚刚出现，而是早在我们以前的人中间就有了；他们也许未曾作出足够严格的规定，便确立了出于他们的淳朴和无知而产生的习惯。”↩︎

  37. quadragesima（四旬期）。↩︎

  38. 太4:2；比较出34:28；王上19:8。↩︎

  39. 参见本节末的附注。↩︎

  40. 勒南 (Renan) 正是这样看待这场争论的；他在《马可·奥勒留》(Marc-Aurèle)，第194页，将其视为两种基督教之间的冲突：“le christianisme qui s’envisageait comme une suite du judaisme”（“自视为犹太教之延续的基督教”），与“le christianisme qui s’envisageait comme la destruction du judaisme”（“自视为犹太教之毁灭的基督教”）。↩︎

  41. 莫斯海姆 (Mosheim)（De rebus christ. ante Const. M Com.，第435页及下页）和尼安德 (Neander)（《教会史》初版，1. 518；但德文第2版 I. 512、托里 [Torrey] 译本 I. 298 已不持此说）曾持这种看法。十四日派并无实行这种犹太习俗的任何迹象。赫费勒 (Hefele，I. 87) 也承认这一点；他从前认为这场争论中有三派，其实只有两派。↩︎

  42. 优西比乌没有明确提及举行圣餐，但可以推知。他说（《教会史》[H. E.] V. 23）：“全亚细亚的众教会，遵循更古老的传统（ὡς ἐκ παραδόσεως ἀρχαιοτέρας*，比罗马的传统更古老），认为自己必须在救主逾越节的节期当日（或期间）（ἐπὶ τῆς τοῦ σωτηρίου πάσχα ἑορτῆς*），遵守月之第十四日，即犹太人奉命宰杀逾越节羊羔的那一天；无论这一天落在一周中的哪一日，他们都必须以这一天为准，结束禁食。”↩︎

  43. 殉道者游斯丁 (Justin M.)，《对话》(Dial.)，第111章；爱任纽，《驳异端》(Adv. Haer.) II. 22, 3；德尔图良，《论洗礼》(De Bapt.) 19；奥利金，《马太福音注释》(In Matth.)；埃皮法尼乌 (Epiph.)，《驳异端》(Haer.) XLII。圣保罗最先宣告基督是我们的逾越节羔羊（林前5:7），然而他的同伴路加却将基督的逾越节晚餐记为发生于十四日；而保罗自己关于设立主餐的记载，也与路加一致。↩︎

  44. ιδʹ*=14，quarta decima（第十四）。参见出12:6；利23:5，那里规定在这一天庆祝逾越节。因此有 Τεσσαρεσκαιδεκατῖται*Quartodecimani 之称，更准确的写法是 Quartadecimani（十四日派）。这一派别名称见于364年老底嘉 (Laodicea) 会议、381年君士坦丁堡会议等会议的教规。↩︎

  45. 《哲学论》或《驳一切异端》(Philosph. or Refutat. of all Haeres.) VIII. 18。↩︎

  46. 勒南也持这种看法，见《基督教会》(L’égl. Chrét.)，第445页及下页；但他像鲍尔 (Baur) 一样，使之与第四福音书的年代次序相冲突。他将罗马习俗追溯到克西斯图斯 (Xystus) 和特勒斯福鲁斯 (Telesphorus) 任主教之时，即公元120年。↩︎

  47. 勒南（前引书，第447页）推测，爱任纽和弗洛里努斯 (Florinus) 曾陪同坡旅甲 (Polycarp) 前往罗马。尼安德等人误将此行定于162年。坡旅甲死于155年，见§19，第51页。阿尼凯图斯 (Anicetus) 的主教任期始于154年或更早。↩︎

  48. 见致罗马主教维克托的一封信的残篇，由优西比乌保存于《教会史》(H. E.) 第V卷第24章（海尼兴 [Heinichen] 编，I. 253）。↩︎

  49. καὶ περὶ ἅλλων τινῶν μικρὰ σχόντες*（或 ἔχοντες*）πρὸς ἀλλήλους*↩︎

  50. μὴ τηρεῖν*，即不守尼散月十四日，这从上下文和第23章可以看出。τηρεῖν* 主要指禁食，可能也包括晚间举行圣餐。这是一个表示遵守律法规条的术语。比较约9:16。↩︎

  51. 《教会史》(H. E.) IV. 26。↩︎

  52. 只有《逾越节编年史》(Chrenicon Paschale) 中的少数残篇例外。↩︎

  53. 优西比乌将他的名字拼作 Ἀπολινάριος*（IV. 21及26、27，参见海尼兴版），佛提乌 (Photius) 以及《逾越节编年史》大多数抄本也如此。但拉丁作者将其拼作 Apollinaris。他生活在马可·奥勒留 (Marcus Aurelius，161—180年) 在位期间，是一位护教士，反对尤其盛行于弗里吉亚 (Phrygia) 的孟他努主义 (Montanism)；不可将他与第四世纪活跃于叙利亚老底嘉、同名为 Apollinarius 或 Apollinaris 的父子二人中的任何一位混淆。↩︎

  54. 丁多夫 (Dindorf) 编，I. 13；亦见劳斯 (Routh)《圣教遗篇》(Reliquiae Sacrae) I，第160页。米利根 (Milligan) 在前引书第109页及下页引用并讨论了此文。↩︎

  55. 如果这确是十四日派的真正观点，那么它就确凿地证明：关于基督受死的日期，这一观点与对观福音书的年代次序相合，而魏策尔 (Weitzel) 和施泰茨 (Steitz) 在这一点上是错误的。↩︎

  56. 因为按照阿波利纳里乌斯 (Apolinarius) 的观点，基督作为律法的真正成全，必定是在十四日，即律法所定的逾越节之日受死。↩︎

  57. στασιάζειν δοκεῖ, κατ’ αὐτούς, τὰ εὐαγγέλια*、inter se pugnare（彼此冲突）等语，似乎就是这个意思。也就是说，这以约翰将基督受死之日定在尼散月十四日为前提；然而，这本身就是一个有争议的问题。以对观福音书的年代次序为出发点的反对者，也可以用这一异议反驳他们。↩︎

  58. 《逾越节编年史》所存希波吕托斯 (Hippolytus) 的残篇也提出同样的论证。但耶稣是真正的逾越节羔羊，这一教义是各教会一致承认的。↩︎

  59. 鲍尔 (Baur，第163页及下页) 和图宾根学派 (Tübingen School) 如此主张，是正确的。↩︎

  60. 魏策尔 (Weitzel)、施泰茨 (Steitz) 和莱希勒 (Lechler) 正是如此假定的，以反对鲍尔。↩︎

  61. 见上引《驳斥异端》(Philosoph.) 段落，以及《逾越节编年史》中的残篇。↩︎

  62. 诚然，埃皮法尼乌斯 (Epiphanius) 区分了十四日派 (Quartadecimanians) 内部的不同意见（《驳异端》Haer. L，第1—3章，Contra Quartadecimanas〔反对十四日派〕），但他没有提到吃逾越节羔羊的习俗，也没有提到在基督受死的年代次序上有何分歧。↩︎

  63. 优西比乌 (Eusebius)，《教会史》(H. E.)，V. 23—25。↩︎

  64. Μεγάλα στοιχεῖα* 一语中的“星辰”之义，见《致丢格那妥书》(Ep. ad Diog.) 7；游斯丁 (Justin)，《对话录》(Dial.)，第23章（τὰ οὐράνια στοιχεῖα*）。↩︎

  65. ὁ ἐπὶ τὸ στῆθος τοῦ κυρίου ἀναπεσών*（曾靠着主胸膛的那一位）。参见《约翰福音》1. 3:25；21:20。正如勒南 (Renan) 所承认的（《马可·奥勒留》Marc-Aurèle，第196页，注2），这一称呼表明，波利克拉底 (Polycrates) 承认《约翰福音》的真实性。↩︎

  66. τὸ πέταλον*。关于这一独特的说法，见第1卷，第431页，注1；它可能是比喻祭司的圣洁。↩︎

  67. 优西比乌，V. 24（海尼兴〔Heinichen〕版，1，第250页及后）。↩︎

  68. 那篇伪托居普良 (Cyprian)、反对掷骰者的讲道《论掷骰者》(De Aleatoribus)，很可能出自他手；其语气俨然是一道教皇通谕。↩︎

  69. 见普法夫 (Pfaff) 所发现的第三篇残篇，很可能出自他反对布拉斯图斯 (Blastus) 的著作。参见《全集》(Opera)，施蒂伦 (Stieren) 编，I. 887。↩︎

  70. 尼西亚 (Nicaea) 会议教父致埃及、利比亚 (Libya) 和五城地区 (Pentapolis) 各教会的会议书信（苏格拉底〔Socrates〕，《教会史》H. E.，I，第9章）说：“关于至圣复活节日期意见的一致，我们也有令人欣喜的消息要告诉你们；……东方一切从前与犹太人同时守这节期的弟兄，今后都将遵从罗马人、我们，以及所有从最早的时候就按我们所定日期庆祝复活节的人。”优西比乌记载（《君士坦丁传》Vita Const. III. 19），亚细亚行省尤其承认了这一决议。他认为，只有上帝和君士坦丁 (Constantine) 皇帝才能消除复活节有两个相互冲突的庆祝日期这一弊端。↩︎

  71. Πεντηκοστή*（ἡμέρα*）、Quinquagesima，意为逾越节安息日之后的第五十日，见第I卷，第225页及后。教父在广义上用它指从复活节至圣灵降临节的整个五十日期间，在狭义上则仅指圣灵降临节这一天的节期。↩︎

  72. 《论偶像崇拜》(De Idol.)，第12章；参见《论洗礼》(De Bapt.)，第19章；《使徒宪章》(Const. Apost.)，V. 20。↩︎

  73. 埃尔维拉 (Elvira，即格拉纳达〔Granada〕) 会议于公元306年首次在此意义上使用 Pentecoste 一词，见第43条教规。其后的一周，后来称为 Hebdomadas Spiritus Sancti（圣灵周）。↩︎

  74. ἡ ἐπιφάνεια, τὰ ἐπιφάνια, ἡ θεοφάνεια, ἡμέρα τῶν φώτων*: Epiphania, Theophania, Dies Luminum, Festura Trium Regum 等，即显现节、神显节、光明日、三王节。亚历山大的革利免 (Clement of Alexandria) 最早提到这一节期，说诺斯替派 (Gnostic) 中的巴西里得派 (Basilidians) 每年以此纪念基督受洗（《杂学》Strom. I. 21）。尼安德 (Neander) 推想，他们是从巴勒斯坦的犹太基督徒那里接受这一节期的。金口约翰 (Chrysostom) 经常提到它。↩︎

  76. 《马太福音》2:11。最早的模糊痕迹，也许见于德尔图良 (Tertullian)《驳犹太人》(Adv., Jud.)，第9章：“Nam at Magos reges fere habuit Oriens.”（因为东方通常视博士为君王。）伪经的童年福音书在这一点上没有提供任何虚构故事。↩︎

  79. 《护教篇》(Apol.)，前引，第65—67章（《全集》Opera，奥托〔Otto〕第III版，第I卷，第I部，第177—188页）。所引段落出自第67章。↩︎

  82. Ὁ προεστώς*，即主持聚会的长老或主教。↩︎

  83. ·Τὴν νουθεσίαν καὶ παράκλησιν*.↩︎

  84. Εὐχὰς πέμπομεν*, preces emittimus.（我们献上祷告。）↩︎

  85. 第65章。↩︎

  86. Ὅση δύναμις αὐτῷ*，其意可能是 pro viribus, quantum potest（尽其所能）；或如德尔图良所说的“de pectore”（发自内心）和“ex proprio ingenio”（出于自己的才思）。另一些人误译为 totis viribus，即竭尽全力，或用清晰洪亮的声音。参见奥托，前引书，第187页。然而，这些段落绝不含有任何反对固定祷文的意思；当时这些祷文无疑已在使用，每个敬拜者不用书本也能熟记，其中首要的就是主祷文。第四、第五世纪的全部礼仪文献，都以一种古老得多的礼仪传统为前提。《使徒宪章》第八卷中的祷文，很可能属于该书最古老的部分。↩︎

  93. § 19，ἀναγινώσκω ὑμῖν*（我读给你们听）。但这篇讲道也可能最初是即席讲述，由速记员（ταχυγράφοι*, notarii）记录下来的。见莱特福特 (Lightfoot)，第306页。↩︎

  94. 由布林尼奥斯 (Bryennios) 编辑出版（1875年），亦收入德·格布哈特 (de Gebhardt) 与哈纳克 (Harnack) 编《使徒教父》(Patr. Apost.)，I. 111—143。莱特福特《罗马的圣革利免》(S. Clement of Rome) 附录第380—390页有一篇良好的译文。莱特福特说：“如果《革利免一书》是基督教礼仪最早的预示，那么所谓《革利免二书》便是基督教讲道的第一个范例。”他认为作者是一位主教；哈纳克则认为是一位平信徒，因为作者似乎把自己与长老们区别开来。莱特福特认为他属于哥林多，并据此解释为什么这篇讲道与革利免致哥林多人的书信合订在一起；哈纳克则有力地论证它出自罗马，属于黑马 (Hermas) 的时代和圈子。布林尼奥斯将其归于罗马的革利免（这是完全不可能的），希尔根费尔德 (Hilgenfeld) 则将其归于亚历山大的革利免（这同样不可能）。↩︎

  95. 《致哥林多人书》(Ad Cor.)，第59—61章，由布林尼奥斯发现，并于1875年首次出版。我们在下文第228页及下页载出革利免的祷文。布林尼奥斯于1883年公布的《十二使徒遗训》(Didache) 中的祷文（第9、10章）更为古老，洋溢着原始纯朴的精神。见§68。↩︎

  96. 见第III卷，第517页及后；除那里所列文献外，还应增列普罗布斯特 (Probst，罗马天主教)《最初三个世纪的礼仪》(Die Liturgie der 3 ersten Jahrh.)，图宾根 (Tüb.)，1870年；C. A. 哈蒙德 (C. A. Hammond)《古代礼仪》(Ancient Liturgies，附导论、注释和礼仪词汇表)，牛津及伦敦，1878年。↩︎

  97. 《使徒宪章》(Ap. Const.)，第VIII卷；亦见丹尼尔 (Daniel)、尼尔 (Neale)、哈蒙德等人所编的礼仪集。↩︎

  98. 《使徒宪章》(Const. Apost.)，第VII卷，第47章。亦见丹尼尔《圣诗宝库》(Thesaurus Hymnol.)，第III卷，第4页，那里称之为 ὕμνος ἑωθινός*（晨颂；《亚历山大抄本》也如此），起句为：Δόξα ἐν ὑψίστοις θεῷ*（荣耀归于至高之处的上帝）。参见第II卷，第268页及后。它也称为 hymnus angelicus（天使颂），而 Ter Sanctus（三圣颂，出自《以赛亚书》6:3）后来则被特别称为 hymnus seraphicus（撒拉弗颂）。丹尼尔将前者定于第三世纪，劳斯 (Routh) 则定于第二世纪。它见于《圣经》的《亚历山大抄本》末尾（藏于大英博物馆），也见于蒂申多夫 (Tischendorf) 在其《神圣文献》(Monumenta Sacra) 中重印的《苏黎世诗篇集》；各处文字略有出入。其拉丁文形式通常被追溯至第四世纪普瓦捷的希拉里 (Hilary of Poictiers)。↩︎

  99. 丹尼尔，前引书，第III卷，第5页。部分内容可参见《使徒宪章》(Const. Ap.) VIII. 37。ὕμνος ἑσπερινός* 或 ὕμνος τοῦ λυχνικοῦ*（晚颂或点灯颂）的起句是：

    Φῶς ἱλαρὸν ἁγίας δόξης*

    Ἀθανάτου πατρὸς οὐρανίου*。↩︎

  100. 见优西比乌《教会史》(H. E.) V. 28。↩︎

  101. 见《导师》(Paedag.) III. 12（波特〔Pott.〕版，第311页）；亦见丹尼尔《圣诗宝库》(Thesaurus hymnologicus) III，第3、4页。丹尼尔称它为“vetustissimus hymnus ecclesiae”（教会最古老的圣诗），但《至高荣耀颂》(Gloria in Excelsis) 或可与之争此殊荣。这首诗屡有德文译本：明特尔 (Münter) 译本（载兰巴赫〔Rambach〕《基督教圣歌选》Anthologie christl. Gesänge，I，第35页）；多尔纳 (Dorner) 译本（《基督论》Christologie，I. 293）；福特拉格 (Fortlage) 译本（《基督教古代圣歌》Gesänge christl. Vorzeit，1844年，第38页）；还有哈根巴赫 (Hagenbach) 的押韵译本（《最初三个世纪的教会史》Die K. G. der 3 ersten Jahrh.，第222页及下页）。英文译本可见查尔斯夫人 (Mrs. Charles)《歌声中的基督徒生命之声》(The Voice of Christian Life, in Song)，纽约，1858年，第44页及下页；更贴近原文的译本见《尼西亚前基督教文库》(Ante-Nicene Christian Library)，第V卷，第343页及下页。↩︎

  102. Λειτουργία τῶν κατηχουμένων*, Missa Catechumenorum（慕道者礼仪）。missa 一词（我们的 mass〔弥撒〕一词即由此而来）最早见于奥古斯丁以及公元398年迦太基 (Carthage) 会议的记录。它源于礼拜每一部分结束时遣散会众的用语，相当于 missio, dismissio（遣散）。奥古斯丁（《讲道集》Serm. 49，第8章）说：“请注意，讲道之后便遣散（missa）慕道者；信徒仍留下。”后来，missa 专指圣餐礼。在希腊教会中，λειτουργία* 或 λιτουργία*，即礼仪，与 missa 完全相当。↩︎

  103. Λειτουργία τῶν πιστῶν*, Missa Fidelium.（信徒礼仪。）↩︎

  104. Μή τις τῶν κατηχουμένων, μή τις τῶν ἀκροωμένων, μή τις ἀπίστων, μή τις ἑτεροδόξων*，“慕道者、听道者、不信者、持异端者，一概不得留在这里。”《使徒宪章》(Const. Apost.) viii. 12。参见金口约翰《马太福音讲道》(Hom. in Matt.) xxiii。↩︎

  105. 《论异端的抗辩》(De PraescR. Haer.)，第41章：“Quis catechimenus, quis fidelis, incertum est”（谁是慕道者，谁是信徒，分辨不清——即指异端中间）；“pariter adeunt, pariter orant, etiam ethnici, si supervenerint; sanctum canibus et porcis, margaritas, licet non veras”（他们一同进来，一同祷告，连偶然到来的异教徒也不例外；他们把圣物给狗，把珍珠——虽不是真珍珠——给猪〔因为他们没有真正的圣礼〕），“jactabunt”（他们竟如此抛掷）。但这不适用于一切异端，尤其不适用于摩尼教徒 (Manichaeans)；他们将圣礼的奥秘观念推衍得比大公教会信徒远得多。↩︎

  106. Μύητοι*, initiati（已入门者）＝πιστοί*, fideles（信徒）。↩︎

  107. 博学的耶稣会士埃马努埃尔·冯·沙伊斯特拉特 (Emanuel von Scheistrate) 首先在《古代阐明》(Antiquitas illustrate，安特卫普〔Antw.〕，1678年) 和《论保密规制》(De Disciplina Arcani，罗马〔Rom.〕，1685年) 中使用这一论证；但路德宗学者 W. 恩斯特·滕策尔 (W. Ernst Tentzel) 在《论保密规制》(Dissert. de Disc. Arcani，莱比锡〔Lips.〕，1683及1692年) 中驳斥了他。然而，滕策尔、卡索邦 (Casaubon)、宾厄姆 (Bingham)、罗特 (Rothe) 和策施维茨 (Zetzschwitz) 将保密规制 (Disc. Arc.) 局限于仪式而排除教义，也是错误的。尤其参见耶路撒冷的区利罗 (Cyril of Jerusalem)《教理讲授》(Katech.)，XVI. 26；XVIII. 32、33。↩︎

  108. 《希伯来书》5:12—14；《哥林多前书》3:1、2。有些教父持此看法，将保密规制 (Disc. Arc.) 追溯至主在《马太福音》7:6中的命令；近代的克雷德纳 (Credner，1844年) 和万丁格 (Wandinger，见韦策尔与韦尔特〔Wetzer and Welte〕新版，I. 1237) 也如此。圣保罗在《哥林多前书》14:23—25暗示，公开礼拜中有外人到场，但未必是在圣餐期间。↩︎

  109. 邦韦奇 (Bonwetsch) 在前引书中如此主张，以反对罗特和策施维茨 (Zetzchwitz)。↩︎

  110. 教会使用 μυστήριον*, σύμβολον, μύησις, μυσταγωγεῖν, κάθαρσις, τελείωσις, φωτισμός*（用于洗礼）等术语，十分明显地表现出这种对应关系。关于希腊的奥秘崇拜，尤其是厄琉西斯 (Eleusinian) 的奥秘崇拜，参见洛贝克 (Lobeck)《阿格劳法穆斯》(Aglaophanus)，柯尼斯堡 (Königsberg)，1829年；普雷勒 (Preller) 在保利 (Pauly)《古典学实用百科全书》(Realencyklop. der Alterthumswissenschaft) 中的数篇文章，III，第83页及后，V，第311页及后；策施维茨，前引书，第156页及后；以及吕布克 (Lübker)《古典时代实用辞典》(Reallex. des class. Alterthums.)，埃勒尔 (Erler) 编，第5版（1877年），第762页。沃伯顿 (Warburton) 和克罗伊策 (Creuzer) 从前认为，希腊奥秘仪式传授一种秘密智慧，尤其是原始启示的传统；洛贝克已驳斥了这一看法。↩︎

  111. 名称：εὐχαριστία, κοινωνία*, eucharistia, communio, communicatio 等，意为感恩、相交、共同领受。↩︎

  112. 《护教篇》(Apol.)，前引，第65、66章。↩︎

  113. Εὐχαριστηθέντος ἄρτου*↩︎

  114. 居普良 (Cyprian) 论及每日献祭；《书信》(Ep.) 54：“Sacerdotes qui Sacrificia Dei quotidie celebramus”（我们这些每日举行上帝之祭的祭司）。安布罗斯 (Ambrose)《致马尔切拉书》(Ep. 14 ad Marcell.) 及最古老的礼仪著作也如此说。但奥古斯丁 (Augustin) 等人证实，各处的遵行方式并不一致。《致雅努阿里乌斯书》(Ep. 118 ad Januar.)，第2章：“Alii quotidie communicant corpori et sanguini Dominico; alii certis diebus accipiunt; alibi nullus dies intermittitur quo non offeratur; alibi sabbato tantum et dominico; alibi tantum dominico.”（有人每日领受主的身体和血；有人在指定的日子领受；有的地方无一日不献祭；有的地方只在安息日和主日献祭；有的地方只在主日献祭。）圣巴西尔 (Basil) 说（Ep. 289）：“我们每周领圣餐四次，即主日、第四日、预备日〔星期五〕和安息日。”金口约翰 (Chrysostom) 则抱怨每日献祭时领餐的人太少。↩︎

  115. 《罗马书》16:16；《哥林多前书》16:20；《哥林多后书》13:12；《帖撒罗尼迦前书》5:26；《彼得前书》5:14。平安之吻在拉丁教会一直延续到十三世纪末，此后移至礼拜结束时，或改为仅用一句话：“Pax tibi et ecclesiae.”（愿你和教会平安。）在俄国教会中，教士在诵读《尼西亚信经》时彼此亲吻，以表示正统信仰与爱心（两者如此经常地分离）在名义上的联合。在科普特教会 (Coptic church) 中，这一原始习俗仍在实行；在一些小的新教宗派中，它也已恢复。↩︎

  116. Προσφορά*.↩︎

  117. Ἐπίκλησις τοῦ Πν. Ἁγ.* 爱任纽 (Irenaeus) 认为，这种 invocatio Spiritus S.（呼求圣灵），连同奉献和感恩，都源于使徒的教导。见施蒂伦 (Stieren) 编本，I，第854页，第2篇残篇。它见于所有希腊礼仪。在《雅各礼仪》(Liturgia Jacobi) 中，其文如下：Καὶ ἐξαπόστειλον ἐφ’ ἡμᾶς καὶ ἐπὶ τὰ προκείμενα δῶρα ταῦτα τὸ Πνεῦμά σου τὸ πανάγιον, τὸ κύριον καὶ ζωοποιόν ... ἵνα ... ἀγιάσῃ καί ποιήσῃ τὸν μὲν ἄρτον τοῦτον σῶμα ἅγιον τοῦ Χριστοὺ σοῦ, καὶ τὸ ποτήριον τοῦτο αἷμα τίμιον τοῦ Χρ. σοῦ, ἵνα γένηται πᾶσι τοῖς ἐξ αὑτῶν μεταλαμβάνουσιν εἰς ἅφεσιν ἁμαρτιῶν καὶ εἰς ζωὴν αἰώνιον, εἰς ἁγιασμὸν ψυχῶν καὶ σωμάτων, εἰς καρτοφορίαν ἔργων ἀγαθῶν*↩︎

  118. Κοινὸς ἄρτος*（普通的饼），游斯丁 (Justin) 如此说；但鉴于其神圣意义，他也称之为非同寻常的饼和饮料。众所周知，使用有酵饼还是无酵饼，后来成为罗马教会与希腊教会之间的一个争论点。↩︎

  119. 这种最简单的分餐用语——“Σῶμα Χριστοῦ*”和“Αἷμα Χρ., ποτήριον ζωῆς*”——见于《使徒宪章》(Apostolic Constitutions) VIII. 13 所载的伪克莱门礼仪，似乎也是最古老的形式。《十二使徒遗训》(Didache) 没有提供分餐用语。↩︎

  120. 会众在主要祷告时以及领圣餐时采取站立的姿势，起初似乎是普遍的惯例。因为在复活之日，站立祷告确实一向是区别于星期五的习俗（奥古斯丁说：“stantes oramus, quod est signunt resurrectionis”，即“我们站立祷告，因为这是复活的标记”）；再者，圣餐在最高的意义上是一种欢庆喜乐的仪式；最后，游斯丁明言：“于是我们全体起立祷告。”十二世纪以后，跪着领受饼酒渐成通例，并从天主教会传入路德宗与圣公会，而大多数改革宗教会则恢复了原来站立的习俗。坐着领圣餐，是宗教改革以后由苏格兰长老会首先引入的，在美国也十分常见，由执事或长老将饼和杯递给坐在长椅上的领餐者。一个奇特的现象是，教皇领圣餐时采取坐姿；斯坦利主任牧师 (Dean Stanley) 认为，这是最初门徒斜倚或卧席姿势的遗存。见其《基督教制度》(Christ. Instit.)，第250页及后。↩︎

  121. 据奥古斯丁见证，在濯足节 (Maundy-Thursday)，圣餐仍在晚间举行，“tanquam ad insigniorem commemorationem”（仿佛为要作更显著的纪念）。在圣诞之夜、主显节、复活节前夕等大节期，以及禁食期间，也是如此。见安布罗斯《诗篇第118篇讲道》(Serm. viii. in Ps. 118)。↩︎

  122. 《护教篇》(Apol.)，第39章：“偏偏只有基督徒简朴的晚餐室，才招来这样大的喧嚷。我们的筵席，顾名便可知其意义。希腊人称它为爱。无论花费多少，我们以敬虔之名所作的支出都是收益，因为我们以筵席上的美物周济贫乏的人；这不像你们那里，食客以满足放纵的欲望为荣，为了饱餐一顿而卖身，甘受一切可耻的待遇；我们却效法上帝自己，特别尊重卑微的人。既然我们筵席的宗旨是良善的，就当据此考察其余的规矩。它既是宗教敬拜的举动，就不容许任何卑污或失检的行为。赴席者在卧席以前，先品尝向上帝祷告的滋味。所吃的，只够解除饥饿；所喝的，只到合乎贞洁的分量。他们说已经足够，因为记得即使在夜间也必须敬拜上帝；他们谈话，仿佛知道主也是听者之一。洗手、点灯以后，每个人都受邀上前，按自己的能力向上帝唱一首赞美诗，或出自圣经，或出于自己的创作——这足以证明我们饮酒的分量。筵席既以祷告开始，也以祷告结束。我们散席而去，不像成群的作恶者，也不像结伙的游荡者，更不是要去纵欲放荡，而是仍然谨守端庄与贞洁，仿佛我们刚才是在德行的学校，而不是在筵席上。”（译文取自《尼西亚前期教父文库》〔“Ante-Nicene Library”〕。）↩︎

  123. 《致士每拿人书》(Ad Smyrn.)，第7章；针对幻影派 (Docetists)，他们否认 τὴν εὐχαριστίαν σάρκα εἶναι τοῦ σωτῆρος ἡμῶν Ἰ. Χρ., κ.τ.λ.*。又《致以弗所人书》(Ad Ephes.)，第20章：Ὅς*（即 ἄρτος*）ἔστιν φάρμακον ἀθανασίας, ἀντίδοτος τοῦ μὴ ἀποθανεῖν, ἀλλὰ ζῆν ἐν Ἰησοῦ Χριστῷ διὰ παντός.*。这两段在叙利亚文译本中都没有。但第一段为狄奥多勒 (Theodoret)《对话录》(Dial.) III，第231页所引用，因此在他的时代，就连叙利亚教会也必定知道这一段。↩︎

  124. 《第一护教篇》(Apol. I.) 66（奥托〔Otto〕第3版，I，第182页）。这里也已经出现了 μεταβολή* 一词，一些罗马天主教论战家随即以此为化质说 (transubstantiation) 的论据。游斯丁说：Ἐξ ἧς*（即 τροφῆς*）αἷμα καὶ σάρκες κατὰ μεταβολὴν τρέφονται ἡμῶν*, ex quo alimento sanguis et carnes nostae per mutationem aluntur.（我们的血与肉藉着变化从这食物得到滋养。）但按上下文，这绝不是指饼酒本质的转变，而是指它们被领受者的身体吸收同化，或是它们对身体所起的作用，且与将来的复活有关。比较《约翰福音》6:54及后，以及伊格那丢 (Ignatius) 和爱任纽的类似段落。↩︎

  125. 《驳异端》(Adv. haer.) IV. 18，以及其他多处。↩︎

  126. 见普法夫 (Pfaff) 所发现残篇中的第二篇（《爱任纽著作集》〔Opp. Tren.〕，施蒂伦编，卷I，第855页）；马费伊 (Maffei) 和其他罗马天主教神学家毫无根据地断言它是伪作。其中说，基督徒献上圣餐祭以后，便呼求圣灵：ὅπως ἀποφήνῃ τὴν θυσίαν ταύτην καὶ τὸν ἄρτον σῶμα τοῦ Χριστοῦ, καὶ τὸ ποτήριον τὸ αἷμα τοῦ Χρ., ἵνα οἱ μεταλαβόντες τούτων τῶν ἀντιτύπων, τῆς ἀφέσεως τῶν ἁμαρτιῶν καὶ ζωῆς αἰωνίου τύχωσιν.*。↩︎

  127. 《彼得前书》3:20、21。↩︎

  128. 《使徒宪章》(Const. Apost.)，卷V，第14章：Τὰ ἀντίτυπα μυστήρια τοῦ τιμίου σώματος αὐτοῦ καὶ αἵματος*。VI. 30 以及 VII. 25 的一篇圣餐祷文也是如此。希腊教父的其他段落，见施蒂伦，前引书，第884页及下页。另比较布莱克 (Bleek) 大部头《希伯来书注释》(Com. on Heb.) 中对8:5和9:24所作的博学论述。↩︎

  129. 《驳马吉安》(Adv. Marc.) IV. 40；III. 19 也如此。这种解释显然十分接近厄科兰帕迪乌斯 (Œcolampadius) 的解释；他把比喻放在谓语中，并且相当看重德尔图良 (Tertullian) 的权威。但慈运理 (Zwingli) 的看法，即把比喻放在 ἐστι.* 而非谓语中，也见于德尔图良《驳马吉安》I. 14：“Panem qui ipsum corpus suum repraesentat.”（那表征他自己身体的饼。）这两种解释，不过是同一象征理论在语法上的不同形式。↩︎

  130. 《论肉身复活》(De Resur. Carnis)，第8章：“Caro corpore et sanguine Christi vescitur, ut et anima de Deo saginetur.”（肉身以基督的身体和血为食，使灵魂也从上帝得饱足。）《论贞洁》(De Pudic.)，第9章，他把浪子比喻中那肥牛犊解释为主的圣餐，并说：“Opimitate Dominici corporis vescitur, eucharistia scilicet.”（他以主身体的丰美为食，也就是圣餐。）《论祷告》(De Orat.)，第6章：“Quod et corpus Christi in pane censetur”，这句话大概应译为：基督的身体也是藉着饼来理解的（而不是包含在饼中）。↩︎

  131. 因此，他认为搀和是必不可少的。《书信》(Epist.) 63（巴卢兹〔Bal.〕版），第13章：“Si vinum tantum quis offerat, sanguis Christi incipit esse sine nobis; si vero aqua sit sola, plebs incipit esse sine Christo. Quando autem utrumque miscetur et adunatione confusa sibi invicem copitlatur, tunc sacramentum spirituale et cœleste perficitur.”（若有人只献上酒，基督的血便没有我们；若只有水，百姓便没有基督。但当二者搀和，混合为一、彼此结合时，这属灵而属天的圣礼才得以成全。）↩︎

  132. 《马太福音注释系列》(Comment. ser. in Matt.)，第85章（III，第898页）：“Panis iste, quem Dem Verbum〔逻各斯 (Logos)〕corpus suum esse fatetur, verbum est nutritorium animarum, verbum de Deo Verbo procedens, et panis de pani cœlesti ... Non enim panem illum visibilem, quem tenebat in manibus, corpus situm dicebat Deus Verbum, sed verbum, in cuius mysterio est panis ille frangendus.”（上帝之道宣称为自己身体的这饼，乃是滋养灵魂的道，是从上帝之道而出的道，也是从天上之饼而出的饼……因为上帝之道所称为自己身体的，并不是他手中所拿的那可见的饼，而是道；那饼应当在这道的奥秘中擘开。）随后，他对酒也作同样的解释。奥利金 (Origen) 显然没有超出慈运理的理论，而革利免 (Clement) 则接近加尔文派的看法，即在圣餐中属灵地真实享受基督的生命。↩︎

  133. Προσφορά, θυσία*, oblatio, sacrificium.（奉献、祭献。）↩︎

  134. 同样，在犹太人中，逾越节晚餐的酒杯被称为“祝福的杯”，ποτήριον εὐλογίας* = εὐχαριστίας*。比较《哥林多前书》10:16。↩︎

  135. 《驳异端》(Adv. Haer.) IV，第18章，§ 4：“Verbum〔逻各斯〕quod offertur Deo”（献给上帝的道）；但按另一些抄本，此处应读作：“Verbum per quod offertur”（藉着他献祭的道）——这与上下文更加相合。比较 IV. 17，§ 6：“Per Jes. Christum offert ecclesia.”（教会藉着耶稣基督献祭。）施蒂伦采用“Verbum quod”这一读法，却认为它不是指基督，而是指祷告的话。无论如何，这段话太过晦涩，也太过孤立，不能据此建立一项教义。↩︎

  136. 《致孔基利乌斯书》(Epist. 63 ad Council.)，第14章：“Si Jesus Christus, Dominus et Deus noster, ipse est summus sacerdos Dei Patris et sacrificium Patri seipsum primus obtulit et hoc fieri in sui commemorationem praecepit: utique ille sacerdos vice Christi vere fungitur, gui id, quod Christus fecit, imitatur et sacrificium verum et plenum tunc offert.”（若耶稣基督、我们的主和上帝，自己就是父上帝的大祭司，首先将自己献给父为祭，并吩咐人如此行以纪念他，那么，惟有那效法基督所行之事的祭司，才真正代行基督的职分，并在那时献上真实而完全的祭。）↩︎

  137. 《第一护教篇》(Apol. I.)，第61章（奥托版，I，第164页）。↩︎

  138. Abrenunciatio diaboti.（弃绝魔鬼。）德尔图良：“Renunciare diabolo et pompae et angelis ejus.”（弃绝魔鬼及其浮华与使者。）《使徒宪章》(Const. Apost.)：Ἀποτάσσομαι τῷ Σατανᾷ καὶ τοῖς ἔργοις αὐτοῦ καὶ ταῖς πομπαῖς αὐτοῦ, καὶ ταῖς λατρείαις αὐτοῦ, καὶ πᾶσι τοῖς ὑπ’ αὐτόν*。我们从耶路撒冷的区利罗 (Cyril of Jerusalem) 得知，至少在四世纪，这种弃绝魔鬼的仪式是在洗礼堂前厅举行的，受洗者面向西方，举手作拒斥的姿势，仿佛撒但就在面前（ὡς παρόντι ἀποτάσσεσθε Σατανᾷ*）；有时还伴以吹气，或其他驱逐邪灵的动作。↩︎

  139. Ὁμολόγησις*, professio（信仰告白）。慕道者或跟随祭司诵读信经，或以回答问题的方式作出告白，同时转脸朝东，面向光明。↩︎

  140. 参见史密斯 (Smith) 与奇瑟姆 (Cheetham)，I，第161页所引的权威文献；奥古斯蒂 (Augusti)，前引书（l.c.）所引更为详尽。德尔图良说：“Ter mergitamur”（我们三次浸入水中）。在南方气候下，浸礼是十分自然的。尼西亚时期 (Nicene age) 的洗礼堂是为浸礼而建的，其中许多至今仍存于亚洲、非洲和南欧；所有东方教会也仍坚持这种方式。加鲁奇 (Garrucci)（《基督教艺术史》Storia della Arte Cristiana，I，第27页）说：“Antichissimo e solenne fu il rito d’ immergere la persona nell’ acqua, e tre volte anche it capo, al pronunziare del ministro i tre nomi.”（将人浸入水中的仪式极其古老而庄严；施礼者宣告那三个名时，也将头浸入三次。）舒尔策 (Schultze)（《地下墓窟》Die Katacomben，第136页）说：“Die Taufdarstellungen vorkonstantinischer Zeit, deren Zahl sich auf drei beläuft, zeigen sämmtlich erwachsene Täuflinge, in zvei FälIen Knabent von etwa zwölf Jahren, im dritten Falle einen Jüngling. Der Act wird durch Untertauchen vollzogen.”（君士坦丁以前的洗礼图像共有三幅，所描绘的受洗者全是已长成的人，其中两幅是约十二岁的男孩，第三幅是一位青年。洗礼是以浸入水中的方式施行的。）斯坦利座堂主任牧师 (Dean Stanley) 喜欢用生动而夸张的笔法，描绘教父时期的浸礼，与现代的洒水礼作对照。他说：“洗礼不只是沐浴，而是猛然浸入——全身没入深水，如同跳进波涛翻滚的海洋或奔流的江河；波浪一时淹没沐浴者的头，他随后又浮现出来，仿佛从片刻的坟墓中出来；或者，它是一次骤然冲淋——水从大容器中倾泻而下，流遍全身，使受洗者如同被裹在飞瀑的水帘之中。这正是使徒们如此着重强调的仪式部分。在他们看来，这好比埋葬从前的旧我，而新的自我重新兴起。”《基督教制度》Christian Institutions（1881年），第9页。参见沙弗 (Schaff)，前引书，第41页及下页。↩︎

  141. 《书信》Ep. I. 41，答希斯帕拉 (Hispala) 主教利安德 (Leander)。托马斯·阿奎那 (Thomas Aquinas)（《神学大全》Summa Theol.，第IV卷，第615叶，米涅版）赞同地引用此信，但更倾向于 trina immersio（三次浸水），因为它表达了“triduum sepulturus Christi et etiam Trinitas personarum”（基督埋葬的三日，以及三个位格的三一性）。↩︎

  142. 《俄罗斯正教教理问答》将洗礼定义为：“一种圣礼，在其中，信的人奉父、子、圣灵上帝的名，身体三次浸入水中，向属肉体的罪恶生活死去，并由圣灵重生，进入属灵而圣洁的生活。”至于婴孩，通常是将身体其余部分浸入水中，再把水浇在头上，以完成这一仪式。一位希腊教会的祭司曾如此告诉我。↩︎

  143. 浇水礼或灌水礼是罗马天主教目前的做法。它最早见于罗马地下墓窟的图画，其中一幅位于卡利克斯图斯 (Calixtus) 墓地，德·罗西 (De Rossi) 将其定为第二世纪的作品。“值得注意的是，在保存至今的几乎所有最早的洗礼图像中，所特别描绘的都是这一动作［从容器中将水浇在身上］。”马里奥特 (Marriott)，载史密斯与奇瑟姆，I，第168页。但绘画艺术只能表现这一行动的一部分，不能表现整个过程；在所有地下墓窟的图画中，受洗者都双脚站在水里，并且脱去衣服，仿佛准备接受全身或部分身体的浸礼。↩︎

  144. “Baptismus clinicorum”（病榻洗礼；κλινικοί*，源自 κλίνη*，意为床）。Clinicus 或 grabbatarius 指在病榻上受洗的人。↩︎

  145. 《致马格努斯书》Ep. 69（另一编号为75），ad Magnum。当时尚无教会的裁决，他只能尽其所能回答这个问题。这封书信，是继德尔图良反对婴儿洗礼之后，洗礼论争文献中最早的文献。居普良 (Cyprian) 引用（第12章）旧约中几处将“洒水”说成洁净行动的经文（结36:25、26；民8:5–7；19:8–13），然后得出结论：“由此可见，洒水也与使人得救的洗濯同样有效（adspersionem quoque aquae instar salutaris lavacri obtinere）；当这事在教会中施行，领受者和施行者的信仰都纯正无缺时（ubi sit et accipieatis et dantis fides integra），一切便都有效，并可藉着主的威严和信仰的真实而成全、完善。”然而，在同一封书信中，居普良否认异端和分裂派以任何方式施行的洗礼有效。参见下文§74。↩︎

  146. 新凯撒利亚会议 (Council of Neo-Caesarea)（314年以后）第十二条教规规定：“凡受过病榻洗礼的人，不得晋升祭司职，因为他的信仰［告白］不是出于自由选择，而是出于迫不得已（ἐξ ἀνάγκης*，即惧怕死亡），除非他后来在热心和信仰上表现卓越，或缺乏［合适的］人选。”这条教规被收入《教会法大全》Corpus jur. can.，c. 1 Dist. 57。参见黑费勒 (Hefele)，《大公会议史》Conciliengesch，I，第249页（第2版）。↩︎

  147. 据瓦拉弗里德·斯特拉波 (Walafrid Strabo)（De Rel. Eccl.，第26章），浇水礼和洒水礼在第九世纪仍属例外；但随着婴儿洗礼的传播，它们作为最方便的方式，尤其适合北方气候，便逐渐发展，到十三世纪末已在西方普遍采用。托马斯·阿奎那（卒于1274年）说，虽然以浸水方式施洗可能更为稳妥，但浇水和洒水也可以采用（《神学大全》Summa Theol.，第III部，第LXVI问，De Rapt.，第7条；米涅版，第IV卷，第614叶）：“Si totum corpus aquâ non possit perfundi propter aquae paucitatem, vel propter aliquam aliam causam, opportet caput perfundere, in quo manifestatur principium animalis vitae.”（如果因为水少或其他缘故，不能用水浇遍全身，就应当浇头，因为生命的本原显明于头部。）在爱尔兰，洒水礼似乎很早就与浸礼并行。“现存最早的爱尔兰洗礼仪文规定三次浸水，也可改用洒水；不过，这篇仪文的编撰明显受到罗马的强烈影响。”F. E. 沃伦 (F. E. Warren)，《凯尔特教会的礼仪与仪式》The Liturgy and Ritual of the CeItic Church，牛津（克拉伦登出版社），1881年，第65页。诺曼·福克斯 (Norman Fox) 教授及其他浸礼宗作者认为：“若非迷信地以为洗礼是得救所必需的，人们决不会想到婴儿洗礼，也不会想到用浇水和洒水施洗。”但这种观念在教父中和希腊教会中流行的程度，丝毫不亚于罗马教会；反之，多数实行洒水礼的新教教会却拒绝这种观念。

    路德 (Luther) 曾试图恢复浸礼，但未奏效。加尔文 (Calvin) 对这一问题的看法与托马斯·阿奎那相近，但他更进一步，宣布用水的方式无关紧要。《基督教要义》Inst. IV，第15章，§19：“受洗者是全身浸入（mergatur totus），是三次还是一次，或只将水浇在（infusa）或洒在他身上（aspergatur），都无关紧要（minimum refert）；应当任凭各教会按各国的不同情形自由决定。然而，施洗一词本身的意思就是浸入（mergere）；而且，古代教会实行浸礼，是确定无疑的。”多数新教徒赞同加尔文，惟浸礼宗除外；他们恢复了古代的做法，但只是部分恢复（以一次代替三次浸水），并不接受教父关于洗礼重生、婴儿洗礼和洗礼为得救所必需的观念。他们认为洗礼仅是象征，表明重生和归信已经发生。↩︎

  148. 德尔图良称之为“principals crimen generis humani”（人类首要的罪行；《论偶像崇拜》De idol.，第1章），居普良则称之为“summum delictum”（最大的罪过；《书信》Ep. x.）。↩︎

  149. Ἀνάδοχοι*, sponsores, fideijussores.（担保人、保证人。）↩︎

  150. 教父用来表达洗礼教义的术语有：ἀναγέννησις*, παλιγγενεσία*（以及 λουτρὸν παλιγγενεσίας*，多3:5），θεογένεσις*、regeneratio, secunda 或 spiritualis nativitas, renascentia（重生、第二次或属灵的出生、再生）；还有 φωτισμός*, φώτισμα*, illuminatio（光照），σφραγίς*, signaculum（印记），μύησις*, μυσταγωγία*（进入奥秘，即圣礼的入门礼）。象征几乎被等同于事物本身。↩︎

  151. “Non defectus（或 privatio）, sed contemtus sacramenti damnat.”（使人被定罪的，不是未能领受或缺少圣礼，而是藐视圣礼。）这就为贵格会信徒 (Quakers)、未受洗的儿童，以及怀着得救愿望而死的蒙拣选的异教徒，敞开了得救之门。↩︎

  152. Procrastinatio baptismi.（延迟洗礼。）↩︎

  153. 因此，《使徒宪章》Apost. Constit.，VI. 15的作者，不赞成那些说如下话的人：ὅτι ὅταν τελευτῶ, βαπτίζομαι, ἵνα μὴ ἁμαρτήσω καὶ ῥυπανῶ τὸ βάπτισμα*（等我临终时再受洗，免得我犯罪，玷污洗礼）。↩︎

  154. 《论悔改》De Paenitientia.↩︎

  155. 《论善工与施舍》De Opere et Eleemosynis.↩︎

  156. 路1:4（κατηχήθης*）；徒18:25（κατηχημένος*）；参罗2:18；林前14:19；加6:6；来5:12。动词 κατηχέω* 的意思是：1）回响；2）口头教导；3）在基督教作者笔下，指教导宗教的基本知识。↩︎

  157. Κατηχηταί*, doctores audientium（听道者的教师）。这一称谓指一种职能，并非特殊的职分或阶层。↩︎

  158. Κατηχούμενοι*, ἀκροαταί*, auditores, audientes（慕道者、听道者）。↩︎

  159. Ἀκροώμενοι*，或 audientes（听道者）；γονυκλίνοντες*，或 genuflectentes（跪拜者）；以及 φωτιζόμενοι*，或 competentes（候洗者）。杜康热 (Ducange)、奥古斯蒂、尼安德 (Neander)、赫夫林 (Höfling)、黑费勒（在其《大公会议史》Conciliengesch. 第1版中如此，但第2版第I卷第246、249页有所修正）、策施维茨 (Zezschwitz)、赫尔佐格 (Herzog) 及许多其他人，都采取这一分类。博纳 (Bona) 和宾厄姆 (Bingham) 甚至另加第四类（ἐξωθούμενοι*）。但这种人为的分类（如丰克博士 (Dr. Funk) 在前引书中所指出的），是由于误解了新凯撒利亚会议（314至325年之间）第五条教规而产生的；该教规提到一位 γόνυ κλίνων*，但他代表的是一类悔罪者，而非慕道者。苏伊策 (Suicer)、迈尔 (Mayer) 和魏斯 (Weiss) 只承认两类，即 audientes 和 competentes。丰克主张，候洗者（φωτιζόμενοι*, companies 或 electi baptizandi）已经被列入信徒（fideles）之中，而慕道者只有一类。↩︎

  160. 埃尔维拉会议 (Council of Elvira)，第42条教规。↩︎

  161. 《使徒宪章》Const. Apost. VIII. 32。↩︎

  162. Σφραγίς*, χρίσμα*, confirmatio obsignatio, signaculum.（印记、膏抹、坚振、盖印、印记。）↩︎

  163. 《演说》Orat. XL。↩︎

  164. 参见第I卷，第469页及下页。这一事实无法得到确凿的证明，却有很强的可能性作为根据。浸礼宗否认这一点。尼安德也否认，但他赞成婴儿洗礼的做法，认为它出于基督教的精神。↩︎

  165. 《与特利丰对话》Dial. c. Tr.，第43章。↩︎

  166. 《护教篇》Apol.，前引处，第15章（奥托版，1，第48页）：οἱ ἐκ παίδων ἐμαθητεύθησαν τῷ Χριστῷ*/（自幼就作了基督门徒的人）。↩︎

  167. 《驳异端》Adv. Haer. II. 22，§4：“Omnes venit per semetipsum salvare; omnes, inquam qui per cum renascuntur in Deum, infantes et parvulos et pueros et juvenes et seniores. Ideo per omnem venit aetatem, et infantibus infans factus, sanctificans infantes; in parvulis parvulus, sanctificans hanc ipsam habentes aetatem; simul et exemplunt illis pietatis effectus et justitae et subjectionis, in juvenibus juvenis,”等等。（他来，要藉着自己拯救所有人；我说的所有人，是指一切藉着他重生归向上帝的人，包括婴儿、幼童、儿童、青年和老年人。因此，他经历了每一个年龄阶段；为婴儿成为婴儿，使婴儿成圣；在幼童中成为幼童，使处于这一年龄的人成圣，同时也为他们成为敬虔、公义和顺服的榜样；在青年中成为青年。）尼安德讨论这一段时指出：“这一观念以基督教最内在的实质为根基，在基督徒的情感中日益突出，由此产生了婴儿洗礼的做法。”（I，第312页，波士顿版。）↩︎

  168. 爱任纽 (Irenaeus) 说到“重生的洗”，以及“重生归向上帝的洗礼”，τὸ βάπτισμα τῆς εἰς θεὸν ἀναγεννήσεως*（《驳异端》Adv. Haer.，前引处，21，§1）；他将使徒受命施洗等同于 potestas regenerationis in Deum（使人重生归向上帝的权柄；III. 17，§1）；他说，基督降到阴间，使古代的族长重生（III，第22章，§4；“in sinum suum recipiens pristinos patres regeneravit eos in vitam Dei”，即“将古时的列祖接入自己怀中，使他们重生，进入上帝的生命”），他所指的可能就是洗礼（依照黑马 (Hermas)、亚历山大的革利免 (Clement of Alexandria) 等人的想象）。关于爱任纽各处相关文字的考察，可参阅鲍尔斯 (Powers) 的文章；他得出的结论是（前引文，第267页）：“爱任纽每逢提到他屡次所说的重生，都隐含着洗礼。”↩︎

  169. 在《罗马书注释》Ep. ad Rom.（《著作集》Opera，第IV卷，第1047栏，米涅版；或第IV卷，第565页，德拉吕版）中说：“Pro hoc et Ecclesia ab apostolis traditionem suscepit, etiam parvulis baptismum dare.”（因此，教会也从使徒领受了给婴孩施洗的传统。）在《利未记讲道集》Levit. Hom. VIII.（米涅版，II，第496页）中，他说，“secundum Ecclesiae observantiam”（按照教会的惯例），也给婴孩（etiam parvulis）施洗。参较他的《马太福音注释》Com. in Matt. XV.（III，第1268页及后）；在那里，他似乎从基督为小孩子祝福的榜样推论出这一习俗。据我所知，奥利金 (Origen) 的著作中，没有一处明言他自己在幼年（185年或稍后）受洗；但从他出身于基督徒家庭，并在幼年受基督徒父母的宗教教导这一点（据优西比乌 (Eusebius)《教会史》H. E. VI. 19：τῷ Ὠριγένει τὰ τῆς κατὰ Χριστὸν*διδασκαλία*ς ἐκ προγόνων ἐσώζετο*），结合埃及的习俗，可以推断此事很有可能。参较雷德彭宁 (Redepenning)，《奥利金》Origenes，I，第49页。要证明他没有在婴儿时期受洗，当然会更加困难。他的神学体系包含柏拉图关于个体灵魂在历史以前堕落的观念，因此很容易为婴儿洗礼留出地位。但居普良 (Cyprian) 和奥古斯丁的神学，却将婴儿洗礼与亚当在历史中的堕落，以及由此而来的遗传性败坏和罪责联系起来。↩︎

  170. “Quid festinat innocens aetas ad remissionem peccatorum?”（无辜的年纪，何必急于求得罪的赦免？）这里的“innocens”（无辜）只能按相对的意义理解；因为德尔图良在别处确实教导一种 vitium originis，即遗传的罪与罪责，虽然不如奥古斯丁那样明确清楚。↩︎

  171. 后来于418年召开的迦太基 (Carthage) 会议更进一步，颁布道：“item placuit, ut quicunque parvulos recentes ab uteris matrum baptizandos negat ... anathema sit.”（又议决：凡否认刚从母腹出生的婴孩应当受洗者……当受咒诅。）↩︎

  172. 《论洗礼》De Bapt.，第15章。另参亚历山大的革利免，《杂学》Strom. I，第375页。↩︎

  173. 见第162页。有些罗马天主教神学家（莫尔肯库尔 (Molkenkuhr) 和蒂扎尼 (Tizzani)，据黑费勒 (Hefele)，第121页引述）认为，像菲尔米利安 (Firmilian) 那封如此不敬的书信（居普良《书信集》中的第75封）不可能是真实的历史文献，而教皇司提反 (Stephen) 与圣居普良之间争论的整个故事，必定都是捏造的！这是教条与事实的对抗。↩︎

  174. 据希波吕托 (Hippolytus)（《驳斥一切异端》Philosoph.）说，在卡利斯图 (Callistus，公元218—223年) 以前，尚无给异端者重新施洗的做法。居普良并不否认罗马习俗的古老，却力言真理胜于习俗（“quasi consuetudo major sit veritate”，即“仿佛习俗竟大于真理”）。黑费勒，I，第121页。司提反的书信已经散佚，我们只能从他的反对者那里了解他的立场。↩︎

  175. 除非主张：在大公教会共融之外领受的洗礼恩典并无益处，反而增加罪责（如同异教徒所拥有的知识一样），只有当异端者本人归信并按正规领受坚振礼后，这恩典才生效。这是奥古斯丁的看法；见施泰茨 (Steitz)，前引文 l. c.，第655页及下页。↩︎

  176. “Pseudochristum, pseudoapostolum, et dolosum operarium.”（假基督、假使徒和诡诈的工人。）菲尔米利安，《致居普良书》Ad Cyp.，近末尾处（Ep. 75）。黑费勒（I，第120页）十分温和地将司提反这种不合基督徒身份的偏狭称为“eine grosse Unfreundlichkeit”（极大的不友善）。↩︎

  177. 奥古斯丁就这样误解了《路加福音》14:22、23的“Coge intrare”（勉强人进来），将其当作逼迫的根据（《致博尼法修书》Ep. ad Bonifac.，第6章）。倘若这位希坡 (Hippo) 的圣洁主教预见到他的释经所产生的可怕后果，他必定会惊恐地退避。↩︎
